For the purpose of our proofs, we need to define a new family of intermediate efficiencies. They provide the maximum possible efficiency, when considering only a particular instance
\alpha\geq 0
of the generalized second laws. For any
\alpha\in[0,\infty)
, let us denote

\displaystyle\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{\alpha}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}^{1})=\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}}\eta(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1},W_{\textup{ext}})\quad\text{s.t.}\penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle F_{\alpha}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\geq F_{\alpha}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),

(316)

\displaystyle\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{0})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{1}),

(317)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}}=\varepsilon|E_{j}\rangle\langle E_{j}|_{\textup{W}}+(1-\varepsilon)|E_{k}\rangle\langle E_{k}|_{\textup{W}},

(318)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{M}}=\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}.

(319)

See Eq. (
56
) for definition of
F_{\alpha}
. We denote
\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}=\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow\infty}\eta_{\alpha}^{\textup{qm}}
. The condition Eq. (
317
), is always satisfied when all the second laws are satisfied. We add the condition as a constraint here, since we will need it in order to write the efficiency
\eta
in the form of Eq. (
315
).

F.1.2
Final correlations do not allow the surpassing of Carnot efficiency

Here, we show that Carnot efficiency cannot be surpassed in a quasi-static heat engine even when we allow arbitrary final correlations in the final state
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
. This can be done in the following steps:

1.

Using the definitions of generalized efficiency (allowing correlations) in Eq. (
311
) and generalized intermediate efficiencies in Eq. (
316
), we prove an inequality between
\eta^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
and
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{\alpha}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
, for all
\alpha\geq 0
. This is done in Lemma
17
. From this, we also conclude that
\eta^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\leq\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
.

2.

On the other hand, we show that for any final state of the cold bath, machine and battery
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
, the generalized intermediate efficiency for
\alpha=1
only increases, if we consider the tensor product of the marginals
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
. In other words,
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})\leq\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1})
. One can intuitively see why this is true: it comes from the fact that the von Neumann entropy is subadditive, therefore the final state
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1}
contains more entropy than
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
. Therefore according to the
\alpha=1
second law, one can potentially draw more work by going to the state
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1}
instead of a correlated state
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
.

3.

Since the argument for
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1})
is of tensor product form, Assumption (iii) holds as before, and therefore the efficiency only depends on the final state of the cold bath
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
. This means that Eq. (
316
) for
\alpha=1
reduces to Eq. (
65
). Lastly, by using Lemma
22
, this allows us to further show in Lemma
19
that even by allowing correlations in
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
, the efficiency
\eta^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
can never surpass the Carnot value.

Firstly, let us fix the following notation:
for an
R
-partite state
\rho_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}
, define the uncorrelated counterpart

\underline{\rho_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{R}\rho_{\textup{A}_{i}}.

(320)

Comparing
\rho_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}
and
\underline{\rho_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}}
, one will see that each subsystem has the same reduced state, but the global state is different. Another useful thing is to note that if one is given a Hamiltonian which does not contain any interaction terms between each subsystem, i.e.

\hat{H}_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}=\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{R}{\mathbbm{1}}_{\textup{A}_{1}}\otimes\cdots\hat{H}_{\textup{A}_{i}}\cdots{\mathbbm{1}}_{\textup{A}_{R}},

(321)

then we may conclude that

\displaystyle\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}\rho_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{A}_{i}}\rho_{\textup{A}_{i}})=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{A}_{i}}\underline{\rho_{\textup{A}_{i}}})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}\underline{\rho_{\textup{A}_{1}\textup{A}_{2}\ldots\textup{A}_{R}}}).

(322)

Lemma 17
.

For all
\alpha\geq 0
and all states
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{Hot}\textup{MW}}
,

\eta^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\leq\eta_{\alpha}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),

(323)

where
\eta^{\textup{qm}}
and
\eta_{\alpha}^{\textup{qm}}
are defined in Eqs. (
311
) and (
316
) respectively.

Proof.

For every
\alpha\geq 0
, Eq.
F_{\alpha}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\geq F_{\alpha}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
in Eq. (
316
) is a necessary condition for the transformation
\rho^{0}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\rightarrow\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
to occur under an energy preserving unitary with the aid of a catalyst
[
7
]
. Energy preserving unitaries also preserve the average energy and thus the Eq.
\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{0})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{1})

in Eq. (
316
) is also a necessary condition.
If a unitary
U(t)
satisfies the conditions in Eq. (
311
), then by the second laws it satisfies Eq. (
316
) for any particular
\alpha\geq 0
.
As a consequence of these observations, the set of allowed unitaries
U(t)
in Eq. (
311
) is a subset of allowed unitaries facilitating the catalytic thermal operation which transforms
\rho^{0}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
to
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
in Eq. (
316
).
∎

Lemma 18
.

For any final state
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
, consider the quantity
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})
defined in Eq. (
316
). Consider the optimization problem

\displaystyle a(\rho_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}^{1}):=\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}}\eta(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1},W_{\textup{ext}})\quad\text{s.t.}\penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}}),

(324)

\displaystyle\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{0})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{1}),

(325)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}}=\varepsilon|E_{j}\rangle\langle E_{j}|_{\textup{W}}+(1-\varepsilon)|E_{k}\rangle\langle E_{k}|_{\textup{W}},

(326)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{M}}=\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}.

(327)

Then,
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})=a(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})
.

Proof.

We begin by noting that the free energy
F_{1}
can be written as

F_{1}(\rho,\tau^{h})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}\rho)-\frac{1}{\beta_{h}}S(\rho),

(328)

where
\langle\hat{H}\rangle_{\rho}:=\textup{tr}[\hat{H}\rho]
, and
S(\rho)=-\textup{tr}(\rho\ln\rho)
is the von Neumann entropy, while
\tau^{h}
is the thermal state at inverse temperature
\beta_{h}
for the Hamiltonian
\hat{H}
. Also, let us recall that
W_{\textup{ext}}=E_{k}^{\textup{W}}-E_{j}^{\textup{W}}>0
where
E_{j}^{\textup{W}}
is a constant.

Next, we consider the free energies
F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
and
F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
respectively, and how they relate to
W_{\textup{ext}}
.
First of all, note that the quantity
F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
is simply a constant that does not depend on
W_{\textup{ext}}
. This is because

\displaystyle F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})

\displaystyle=F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}})+F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{M}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{M}})+F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{W}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{W}})

(329)

\displaystyle=F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}})+F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{M}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{M}})+\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{W}}\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0})-\beta_{h}^{-1}S(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0})

(330)

\displaystyle=F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}})+F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{M}}^{0},\tau^{h}_{\textup{M}})+E_{j}^{\textup{W}},

(331)

where the first two terms do not depend on the battery Hamiltonian at all, while in the last equality we have made use of the fact that
\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}=\left|E_{j}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{j}\right|_{\textup{W}}
.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})

\displaystyle=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{ColdMW}}\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})-\beta_{h}^{-1}S(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})

(332)

\displaystyle=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{Cold}}\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})+\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{M}}\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1})+\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{W}}\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1})-\beta_{h}^{-1}S(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})

(333)

\displaystyle=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{Cold}}\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})+\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{M}}\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1})-\beta_{h}^{-1}S(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})+\varepsilon E_{j}^{\textup{W}}+(1-\varepsilon)E_{k}^{\textup{W}}.

(334)

Note that again,
\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{Cold}}\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
and
\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{M}}\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1})
do not depend on the battery Hamiltonian and therefore do not depend on
E_{k}^{\textup{W}}
. Similarly,
S(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})
depends only on the eigenvalues of the state, and is independent of
E_{k}^{\textup{W}}
. Since
\varepsilon\in[0,1)
, we may conclude the following:
F(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
is a continuous function that strictly increases w.r.t.
E_{k}^{\textup{W}}
, and therefore it also strictly increases w.r.t.
W_{\textup{ext}}
.

To prove this lemma, it suffices to show that the supremum over
W_{\textup{ext}}
in Eq. (
316
) for
\alpha=1
has to be achieved when
F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}})
. We prove this by contradiction. Suppose that
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
achieves the supremum for
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}
, and for this value of
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
,
F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})>F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}})
. Since we know that
F(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
strictly increases w.r.t.
W_{\textup{ext}}
, there must exist an
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}>\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
such that
F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\geq F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{MW}})
. Furthermore, since by Eq. (
315
) we know that the efficiency is monotonically increasing w.r.t.
W_{\textup{ext}}
as well, it follows that
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}
achieves a higher value of efficiency compared to
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
while satisfying the required constraints at the same time. This is a contradiction, and therefore we conclude that the optimization for
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}
can be simplified to
a(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})
, where the constraint on
F_{1}
holds with equality.
∎

Lemma 19
.

For any final state
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{Hot}\textup{MW}}
, and any Hamiltonian of the form in Eq. (
37
),
then for perfect or near perfect work extraction (see Defs.
4
and
5
), we have

\eta^{\textup{qm}}\left(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \overset{\textup{(1)}}{\leq}\penalty\ \penalty\ \eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}\left(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \overset{\textup{(2)}}{\leq}\penalty\ \penalty\ \eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}\left(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \overset{\textup{(3)}}{=}\penalty\ \penalty\ \eta^{\textup{mac}}\left(\underline{\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \overset{\textup{(4)}}{\leq}\penalty\ \penalty\ 1-\frac{\beta_{h}}{\beta_{c}},

(335)

with equality in
(2)
iff
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}=\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}}
.
The quantities
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}
and
\eta^{\textup{mac}}
are defined in Eq. (
316
) and Eq. (
65
) respectively.

Proof.

Note that inequality (1) is a direct consequence of Lemma
17
, while inequality (4) holds because of Lemma
6
. It remains to prove inequalities (2) and (3).

Proof of inequality (2):

Using the definition in Eq. (
316
) together with Lemma
18
, let us compare the quantities

\displaystyle\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}^{1})=\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}}\eta(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1},W_{\textup{ext}})\quad\text{s.t.}\penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),

(336)

\displaystyle\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{0})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{1}),

(337)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}}=\varepsilon|E_{j}\rangle\langle E_{j}|_{\textup{W}}+(1-\varepsilon)|E_{k}\rangle\langle E_{k}|_{\textup{W}},

(338)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{M}}=\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}},

(339)

and

\displaystyle\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\underline{\rho_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}^{1}})=\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}}\eta(\underline{\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}},W_{\textup{ext}})\quad\text{s.t.}\penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),

(340)

\displaystyle\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{0})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{1}),

(341)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}}=\varepsilon|E_{j}\rangle\langle E_{j}|_{\textup{W}}+(1-\varepsilon)|E_{k}\rangle\langle E_{k}|_{\textup{W}},

(342)

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{M}}=\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}.

(343)

We first make the following observations:

•

By our definition of
\underline{\rho_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}^{1}}
, we have that
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}=\underline{\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}}
. Therefore, the term
\Delta C
in Eq. (
315
) which is only a function of the reduced state on the cold bath is the same for both efficiencies in Eq. (
336
) and Eq. (
340
). Therefore, to compare the efficiencies, we need only to compare the value of
W_{\textup{ext}}
that satisfies the free energy constraint in both optimization problems.

•

In
[
65
]
(pg. 395) it has been proven that the von Neumann entropy is subadditive

S(\rho_{AB})\leq S\left(\underline{\rho_{AB}}\right),

(344)

with equality iff
\rho_{AB}=\underline{\rho_{AB}}
. Furthermore, since
\hat{H}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
does not contain interaction terms, as we have demonstrated earlier in Eq. (
322
),

\displaystyle\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{ColdMW}}\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})

\displaystyle=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{\textup{ColdMW}}\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}).

(345)

Thus, by Eq. (
328
) we conclude that

F_{1}(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}})\leq F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),

(346)

with equality iff
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}=\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}}
.

•

For any final state
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
where
\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}=\varepsilon|E_{j}\rangle\langle E_{j}|_{\textup{W}}+(1-\varepsilon)|E_{k}\rangle\langle E_{k}|_{\textup{W}}
, we have seen in the proof of Lemma
18
that
F_{1}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
is a continuous function that strictly increases with
W_{\textup{ext}}
.

With these three observations we can now prove inequality (2). Note that when
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}=\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
, equality holds trivially. Therefore, let us consider the case where
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}\neq\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
.
Suppose
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
achieves the supremum in
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})
, and for such a value of
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
,

F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})>F_{1}(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}).

(347)

We note also that since
F_{1}(\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
strictly increases with
W_{\textup{ext}}
, and therefore there exists some
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}>\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
such that
F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
. Therefore,
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}
is a feasible solution for Eq. (
340
), i.e. it satisfies the constraints in the optimization problem. In conclusion, we have

\displaystyle\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})

\displaystyle=\left[1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C}{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}}\right]^{-1}\leq\left[1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C}{W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\leq\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}).

(348)

Proof of equality (3):

Consider the quantity
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}})
. Since the state
\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}}
takes on a product structure form between all the subsystems now, Assumption (iii) in the beginning of Section
F.1.1
holds again. By Eqns. (
338
) and (
339
), we know that Assumptions (i) and (ii) also hold. Therefore, we know that under these assumptions the efficiency does not depend anymore on the global state
\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}}
, but only
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
. Again comparing the conditions of
\eta^{\textup{mac}}(\underline{\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}})
and
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}})
, we see that they are exactly the same quantity.
∎

Therefore, Lemma
19
tells us that correlations between the final states of the cold bath, machine and battery cannot allow you to achieve an efficiency greater than the Carnot efficiency.

F.1.3
Achievability of Carnot efficiency still depends on more than temperature

Earlier in Section
F.1.2
, we proved in Lemma
19
that Carnot efficiency gives an upper bound to the efficiency of any arbitrary final state
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
. In this section, we want to prove that when
\Omega>1
holds, quasi-static heat engines cannot achieve the Carnot efficiency even when allowing correlations between the final states of the battery and the cold bath. This can be done in the following steps:

•

According to Lemma
19
, Carnot efficiency can be attained only when all the inequalities in Eq. (
335
) are satisfied with equalities. We use this to prove in Lemma
20
that in order to achieve the Carnot efficiency, we may only consider the limit where correlations in the final state vanish. Not only so, the magnitude of these correlations also have to vanish quickly enough in order for Carnot efficiency to be achieved. In particular, we define a parameter
k
which quantifies the amount of correlations, and show that
k
has to vanish faster than the quasi-static parameter
g
, in order to achieve the Carnot efficiency
\eta_{C}
.

•

Next, in Lemma
21
, we show that if the parameter
k
vanishes faster than the quasi-static parameter
g
, then whenever
\Omega>1
, one can derive an upper bound for the intermediate efficiency
\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})
which considers the amount of work extractable by invoking only the generalized second law of
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
. Combining Lemma
20
and Lemma
21
, we conclude in Corollary
3
that when
\Omega>1
,
\eta^{\textup{qm}}\leq\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}\leq\eta_{C}
is strictly upper bounded away from the Carnot efficiency.

Before we begin, let us note that by definition, the initial state
\rho^{0}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{W}}
is diagonal in its energy eigenbasis. Furthermore, the state
\rho^{0}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
is of the form
\rho^{0}_{\textup{Cold}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}}
. Since w.l.o.g. we can assume that
\hat{H}_{\textup{M}}
is proportional to the identity (or called the
trivial Hamiltonian
, see
[
7
]
),
\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}
can always be written as a diagonal state in an energy eigenbasis of its Hamiltonian. Therefore the state
\rho^{0}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
is always diagonal in the energy eigenbasis of the Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}:=\hat{H}_{\textup{Cold}}+\hat{H}_{\textup{M}}+\hat{H}_{\textup{W}}
. Since catalytic thermal operations cannot create coherences
[
7
]
,
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
has to be also diagonal in the energy eigenbasis of
\hat{H}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
.

We observe that any
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
can always be written as

\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}=(1-k^{*})\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}+k^{*}\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{corr}},

(349)

where
k^{*}=\min\{k\in[0,1]|\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}=(1-k)\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}+kQ,Q\geq\mathbf{0}\}
. This means that
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
can be written as a convex combination of two states: one being
\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
, and the other
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{corr}}
containing all other correlations. Note that such a
k^{*}
always exists, in particular,
k=1
is always a feasible solution.

We now define a particular parametrization of the final states,

\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}):=(1-k)\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}+k\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\quad k\in[0,k^{*}]

(350)

where the following holds:

(i)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}=\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}},

(351)

(ii)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}\neq\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},

(352)

(iii)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \rho_{\textup{M}}^{1}=(1-k)\rho_{\textup{M}}^{\textup{no corr}}+k\rho_{\textup{M}}^{\textup{corr}}=\rho_{\textup{M}}^{0}.

(353)

Since in our heat engine, the initial state has no coherences, it suffices to consider
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
which is diagonal in the energy eigenbasis. This implies that
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{no corr}}=\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
is also diagonal in the energy eigenbasis, and therefore the same holds for
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{corr}}
due to Eq. (
350
).
All correlations between the individual systems of cold bath, machine and battery are contained only in
\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
.
Therefore,
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
parametrizes every possible quantum state on
\mathcal{H}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
which is diagonal in the global energy eigenbasis and that returns the machine locally to its initial state after one cycle of the heat engine.
In Eq. (
353
),
\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1}
is the final state of the machine, since the heat engine is cyclic, recall from Section
A
that we require
\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1}=\rho_{\textup{M}}^{0}
.

Lemma 20
.

For every family of states
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})
parametrized by
k
, (see Eqs. (
350
)-(
353
)), if the quantum efficiency
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}
defined in Eq. (
316
) of a quasi-static heat engine achieves the Carnot efficiency

\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}})=1-\frac{\beta_{h}}{\beta_{c}},

(354)

then the following conditions are satisfied:

1)

The state
\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}
is the final state of a quasi-static heat engine (see Def.
6
)

\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}=\tau(g)\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}(g)\otimes\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}}\quad\text{with }g\rightarrow 0^{+}.

(355)

2)

The correlations must vanish sufficiently quickly. That is to say, the parameter
k
in Eq. (
350
)
vanishes more quickly compared to
g
, i.e.

\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{k}{g}=0.

(356)

Proof.

Firstly, suppose that Carnot efficiency is achieved, i.e.
\eta^{\textup{qm}}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1})=1-\frac{\beta_{h}}{\beta_{c}}
. Then according to Lemma
19
, all inequalities in Eq. (
335
) should be satisfied with equality, in particular inequality (4). We have established in Lemma
6
that this equality is achieved in the quasi-static limit, i.e.
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}=\tau_{\textup{Cold}}(g)
where
g\rightarrow 0^{+}
. This implies Condition 1) in the statement of the lemma.

The proof for Condition 2) consists of calculating
W_{\textup{ext}}
for
\alpha=1
in Eq. (
316
) to leading order in
g
and
k
. This
W_{\textup{ext}}
quantity can be later used to evaluate
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}
. We will show that we can write the expression for
\eta_{1}^{\textup{qm}}
into two terms: one term describes the efficiency when there are no final correlations, and the other term is a strictly negative contribution which must vanish in order to achieve the Carnot efficiency. This latter constraint will give us Eq. (
356
).

Let us denote
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}
as the value of battery energy gap
W_{\textup{ext}}=E_{k}^{\textup{W}}-E_{j}^{\textup{W}}
that solves the equation

F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}(g)\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}),\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),

(357)

while
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
as the value that solves the case where
k=0
, i.e.

F_{1}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}(g)\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=F_{1}(\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}).

(358)

Since
\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}=\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}=\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{M}}^{0}(g)\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}
contains no correlations,
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
was given by Eq. (
126
).
According to Lemma
18
, we know that
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}
and
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
are the values of
W_{\textup{ext}}
which solve

\displaystyle\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}}\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1},W_{\textup{ext}})
and
\displaystyle\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}}\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}},W_{\textup{ext}})
respectively.
Solving Eq. (
357
) for
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}
with the aid of Eq. (
328
), we find

W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}=\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}-\chi,

(359)

where
W_{\textup{ext}}
is the solution to Eq. (
358
) when
k=0
, given by Eq. (
126
), while

\chi:=\frac{1}{\beta_{h}}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\left[S(\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})-S\left(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\right)\right].

(360)

Let us first note some properties of
\chi
, which we will later use:

•

Since
S(\cdot)
is subadditive, due to the parametrization of
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
in Eq. (
350
), we have

\chi\geq 0

(361)

with equality iff
\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}}=\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
i.e. iff
k=0
. Therefore, we may conclude that
\frac{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}}{W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}}\geq 1
.

•

We have that

\frac{d}{dk}\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{k=k_{0}}=0

(362)

if and only if

\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k_{0},\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}/N.

(363)

Eqs. (
362
) and (
363
) are direct consequences of the observations:

1) Entropy is strictly concave, i.e.
S\left(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}},\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\right)
is strictly concave in
k\in[0,1]
. Therefore, by Eq. (
363
)
\chi
is strictly convex in
k\in[0,1]
. When the first derivative of the convex function
\frac{d\chi}{dk}=0
, this must be the global minimum
[
66
]
.

2) However, we know that the entropy is uniquely maximized (and therefore
\chi
is uniquely minimized) for the maximally mixed state.

Returning to evaluate the efficiency, we may use Eq. (
315
) to calculate the inverse efficiency,

\displaystyle[\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}})]^{-1}

\displaystyle=1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}})}{W^{\prime}_{\textup{ext}}}

(364)

\displaystyle=1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}})}{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}}\frac{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}}{W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}}

(365)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}})}{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}}.

(366)

The last term in Eq. (
365
) is non-negative because we know the terms
\Delta C(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}),\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}
and
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}
are all non-negative.

With Condition 1), we now know that

1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}})}{W_{\textup{ext}}}=1-\frac{\beta_{h}}{\beta_{c}},

(367)

in the quasi-static limit, and therefore
a necessary condition to achieve the Carnot efficiency is that
\lim_{g\rightarrow 0}\frac{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}}{W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}}=1
also in the quasi-static limit. Using the relation
W_{\textup{ext}}^{\prime}=\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}+\chi
, we have the requirement that

\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})}{\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}(\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g))}=0.

(368)

First, let us observe that
\hat{W}_{\textup{ext}}(\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g))=W_{\textup{ext}}(\beta_{c}-g)
given by Eq. (
113
). The leading order term of
W_{\textup{ext}}(\beta_{c}-g)={\Theta}(g)
as
g\rightarrow 0^{+}
. Therefore, in order to satisfy Eq. (
368
), we must firstly have
\lim_{g\rightarrow 0}\chi=0
. From Eqs. (
350
), (
360
), this implies that we need
k\rightarrow 0
for all
\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
.

Since the numerator and denominator of Eq. (
368
) both go to zero, by L’Hospital rule, to evaluate the limit we need to take the derivative of both terms w.r.t.
g
. Therefore, we expand
\chi
to first order in
k
and
g
. From Eq. (
360
) it follows

\displaystyle\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dk}\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(0),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{k=0}k

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{dg}\chi(0,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{g=0}g+o(gk)+o(k^{2})+o(g^{2})

(369)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dk}\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(0),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{k=0}k+o(gk)+o(k^{2})+o(g^{2}).

(370)

The term
\frac{d}{dg}\chi(0,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{g=0}=0
since when
k=0
,
\chi
will be constant for all
g
.
Next, we note that since Eqs. (
361
) holds, it must be that
\frac{d}{dk}\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(0),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{k=0}\geq 0
. Furthermore, from Eq. (
362
), we have that

\frac{d}{dk}\chi(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(0),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\bigg|_{k=0}\neq 0,

(371)

for all
\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
since by definition
\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}(0,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(0),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})\neq{\mathbbm{1}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}/N
. We can infer that
\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
is not maximally mixed from a few observations, for example: this is true because we have required that the reduced state on the battery is not maximally mixed since we consider near perfect work extraction.

Thus, taking into account
W_{\textup{ext}}(\beta_{c}-g)={\Theta}(g)
, Eq. (
368
) implies Eq. (
356
).
∎

By now, we have established a constraint on how quickly the correlations have to vanish w.r.t.
g
, for the possibility of achieving Carnot efficiency. In the next Lemma
21
, we will show that the constraints given by Eq. (
356
) can be used to derive an upper bound for
\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}
.

Lemma 21
.

If Eqs. (
355
) and (
356
) are satisfied, then the quantity
\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}
can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k,

\displaystyle\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}))

(372)

\displaystyle\leq\left[1+\frac{\beta_{h}}{\beta_{c}-\beta_{h}}\frac{\gamma(1)}{\gamma(\infty)}\right]^{-1}+{\Theta}(f(g))+{\Theta}(k/g)+{\Theta}(g)+{\Theta}(\varepsilon),

(373)

with
\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}f(g)=0
.

Proof.

The main idea of our proof is as follows: we show that if Eqns. (
355
) and (
356
) hold, then we can upper bound
W_{\textup{ext}}
while considering only the
F_{\infty}
condition. This bound differs from the value given when no correlations are present by only a small amount.
Substituting this into the expression for
\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}
, we obtain Eq. (
373
).

Let us begin by analyzing the difference in eigenvalues of the states
\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
and
\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}(k,\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}})

\displaystyle=(1-k)\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}+k\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}

(374)

where
\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
are both diagonal in the energy eigenbasis.
Since
\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
is a mixture of two energy-diagonal states, it is also diagonal. Let us denote its eigenvalues as
[\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}]_{i}
.

As for
\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}
, Eqn. (
355
) gives the explicit form of the state,

\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}=\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}=\tau(g)\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}(g)\otimes\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}}.

(375)

Let us denote its eigenvalues as
[\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}]_{i}
.

We first observe two properties involving trace distance
d(\cdot,\cdot)
:

(P.i)

Consider two states
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
diagonal in the same eigenbasis. Then if
\rho=(1-k)\sigma_{1}+k\sigma_{2}
for some
k\in[0,1]
, then one can conclude that the distance

\displaystyle d(\rho,\sigma_{1})\leq k.

(376)

(P.ii)

For any two states
\rho,\sigma
diagonal in the same basis, with eigenvalues
p_{i},q_{i}
, if their trace distance

d(\rho,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}\|\rho-\sigma\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon,

(377)

then this implies that their eigenvalues cannot differ by more than
\varepsilon
, i.e.
\forall i,|p_{i}-q_{i}|\leq\varepsilon
.
By using this fact, we may first calculate the trace distance between
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
and
\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
, then bound the difference of their eigenvalues.

We find that

\displaystyle d(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1},\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}})

\displaystyle\leq d(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1},\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{no corr}})+d(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{no corr}},\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}})

(378)

\displaystyle\leq k+d(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{\textup{no corr}},\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})+d(\rho_{\textup{M}}^{\textup{no corr}},\rho_{\textup{M}}^{1})+d(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{\textup{no corr}},\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1})

(379)

\displaystyle\leq 4k.

(380)

The first inequality is a triangle inequality that holds for all states. The second inequality holds because of (P.i), and because trace distance is subadditive under tensor product (note that both
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{no corr}}
and
\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
are tensor product states). The third inequality holds because we know
d(\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1},\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{\textup{no corr}})\leq k
and that trace distance decreases under partial trace. By (P.ii), Eq. (
380
) tells us that
\forall i
,

[\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}]_{i}=[\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}]_{i}+o(k).

(381)

With Eq.(
381
), we may relate the
F_{\infty}
quantities for the states
\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}
and
\underline{\rho_{\textup{ColdMW}}^{1}}
. From Eq. (
56
), we have

\displaystyle F_{\infty}\big(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g)

\displaystyle,\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\big)

(382)

\displaystyle=\ln\max_{i}\left\{\frac{[\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}]_{i}}{\tau_{i}}\right\},

(383)

\displaystyle=\ln\max_{i}\left\{\frac{[\underline{\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}]_{i}}{\tau_{i}}\right\}+o(k),

(384)

\displaystyle=F_{\infty}\left(\tau(g)\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}(g)\otimes\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\right)+o(k),

(385)

where we used Eq. (
56
) in the last line.

The next step is to evaluate the restriction on
W_{\textup{ext}}
that satisfies

\displaystyle F_{\infty}(\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}})

\displaystyle\geq F_{\infty}\left(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(k,\rho^{\textup{{no corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}(g),\rho^{\textup{{corr}}}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}),\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\right)

(386)

\displaystyle=F_{\infty}\left(\tau(g)\otimes\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}}(g)\otimes\rho^{1}_{\textup{W}},\tau^{h}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}\right)+o(k),

(387)

for
W_{\textup{ext}}
up to order
o(k)
. Taking into account the additivity of
F_{\infty}
under tensor product, we can rearrange Eq. (
387
) to provide an upper bound on
W_{\textup{ext}}
,

W_{\textup{ext}}\leq\frac{ng}{\beta_{h}}\left[\gamma(\infty)+{\Theta}(f(g))+o(k/g)\right],

(388)

where
\displaystyle\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}f(g)=0
,
\gamma(\infty)
is given by Eq. (
276
). The bound in Eq. (
388
) is achievable since the
F_{\infty}
conditions imposed by Eq. (
386
) are achievable with equality.

Lastly, by using the expression for efficiency in Eqs. (
315
), and substituting
W_{\textup{ext}}
from Eq. (
388
) (with equality for the maximum possible
W_{\textup{ext}}
) followed by
\Delta C
from Eq. (
279
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}>0}\eta(\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}},W_{\textup{ext}})

\displaystyle=\sup_{W_{\textup{ext}}>0}\left(1-\varepsilon+\frac{\Delta C}{W_{\textup{ext}}}\right)^{-1}

(389)

\displaystyle=\left[1+\frac{\beta_{h}}{(\beta_{c}-\beta_{h})}\frac{\gamma(1)}{\gamma(\infty)}\right]^{-1}+{\Theta}(f(g))+o(k/g)+{\Theta}(g)+{\Theta}(\varepsilon).

(390)

Hence using Eqs. (
316
), (
390
) we find Eq. (
373
). Note that in Eq (
373
) we have an inequality, this is due to the fact that in the optimisation problem Eq. (
316
), there is an additional constraint (namely mean energy conservation) which is not taken into account in the derivation of Eq. (
390
).
∎

Finally, the above lemmas allow us to conclude that allowing further correlations in the final state cannot allow quasi-static heat engines to achieve the Carnot efficiency when
\Omega>1
.

Theorem 3
.

[Correlations do not improve efficiency] Suppose that
\Omega>1
. Parametrizing the final state of the heat engine by Eq. (
350
)-(
353
), the quantum efficiency
\eta^{\textup{qm}}
defined in Eq. (
311
) in a quasi-static heat engine is strictly upper bounded by the Carnot efficiency,

\sup_{k\in[0,1],\penalty\ \rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}}}\eta^{\textup{qm}}\left(\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}(k,\rho^{\textup{no corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}},\,\rho^{\textup{corr}}_{\textup{ColdMW}})\right)<1-\frac{\beta_{h}}{\beta_{c}}.

(391)

Proof.

From Lemma
17
, we have that both
\eta^{\textup{qm}}\leq\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}
and
\eta^{\textup{qm}}\leq\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{\infty}
hold. Thus a necessary condition to achieve the Carnot efficiency for a particular
\rho^{1}_{\textup{ColdMW}}
, is that both
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}
and
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{\infty}
are equal to or greater than the Carnot efficiency.

Lemma
20
proves that Eqs. (
355
) and (
356
) are necessary conditions for
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{1}
to achieve the Carnot efficiency. However, when Eqs. (
355
), (
356
) are satisfied, then Lemma
21
provides an upper bound on the efficiency
\eta^{\textup{qm}}_{\infty}
in Eq. (
373
).

Now, suppose
\Omega>1
. Since it is shown in Eq. (
277
) that
\gamma(1)/\gamma(\infty)=\Omega
, plugging this into the leading term appearing in Eq. (
373
)

\left[1+\frac{\beta_{h}}{(\beta_{c}-\beta_{h})}\frac{\gamma(1)}{\gamma(\infty)}\right]^{-1},

(392)

we have that the quantity
\eta_{\infty}^{\textup{qm}}
(and therefore also
\eta^{\textup{qm}}
) is strictly less than the Carnot efficiency
1-\beta_{h}/\beta_{c}
.
∎

F.2
A more general final battery state

For the simplicity of our analysis, we have assumed that the battery is left in the specific final state described in Eq. (
41
), i.e. an amount of work
W_{\textup{ext}}=E_{k}-E_{j}
is extracted, except with failure probability
\varepsilon
that the battery remains in the initial state
\left|E_{j}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{j}\right|_{\textup{W}}
. In this section, we show that this is a simplification which can be removed in general, i.e. the final battery state is allowed to be any state within the
\varepsilon
-ball of
\left|E_{k}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{k}\right|_{\textup{W}}
. In particular, our result that the Carnot efficiency cannot be achieved when
\Omega>1
still holds.

In Lemma
22
, we show that for any final state of the cold bath
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
, allowing a more general final battery state does not affect the amount of work bounded by the
F_{\infty}
condition. We then use this to prove in Theorem
4
that when
\Omega>1
, the Carnot efficiency cannot be achieved even if we allow a more general battery final state.

Lemma 22
.

For any given
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{0},\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
, with
\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}=\left|E_{j}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{j}\right|_{\textup{W}}
, consider the maximum
W_{\infty}^{1}:=E_{k_{1}}-E_{j}
such that
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}\rightarrow\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}
is allowed by the non-increasing
F_{\infty}
condition (Eq. (
55
)) i.e.

D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\|\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{\beta_{h}})+D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}\|\tau_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}})\geq D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\|\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{\beta_{h}})+D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}\|\tau_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}}),

(393)

with

\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}=(1-\varepsilon)\left|E_{k_{1}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{k_{1}}\right|_{\textup{W}}+\varepsilon\left|E_{j}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{j}\right|_{\textup{W}}.

(394)

On the other hand, consider any battery final state

\rho_{\textup{W}}^{2}=(1-\varepsilon)\left|E_{k_{2}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{k_{2}}\right|_{\textup{W}}+\varepsilon\rho_{\textup{W}}^{\textup{junk}},

(395)

where
\rho_{\textup{W}}^{\textup{junk}}
is an energy-diagonal state orthogonal to
\left|E_{k_{2}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{k_{2}}\right|_{\textup{W}}
which may depend on
\varepsilon
, i.e.
\rho_{\textup{W}}^{\textup{junk}}=\sum_{i}p_{i}\left|E_{i}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{i}\right|_{\textup{W}}
with
p_{k_{2}}=0
and
\sum_{i}p_{i}=1
.
Define
W_{\infty}^{2}:=E_{k_{2}}-E_{j}
such that
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}\rightarrow\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\otimes\rho_{\textup{W}}^{2}
is allowed by the non-increasing
F_{\infty}
condition, i.e.

D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\|\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{\beta_{h}})+D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}\|\tau_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}})\geq D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\|\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{\beta_{h}})+D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{2}\|\tau_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}}).

(396)

Then for all
0<\varepsilon\leq\hat{\varepsilon}=\left[1+e^{\beta_{h}(E_{\rm max}-E_{j})}\right]^{-1}
, we have
W_{\infty}^{1}=W_{\infty}^{2}
.

Proof.

Firstly, note that any energy-diagonal state
\rho_{\textup{W}}^{2}
with trace distance
d(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{2},\left|E_{k_{2}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle E_{k_{2}}\right|_{\textup{W}})=\varepsilon
can be written in the form of Eq. (
395
). Rearranging the terms in Eq. (
393
),

\displaystyle D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{1}\|\tau_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}})

\displaystyle\leq D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{W}}^{0}\|\tau_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}})+D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{0}\|\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{\beta_{h}})-D_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}\|\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{\beta_{h}})=:A.

(397)

One can use the definition of
D_{\infty}
in Eq. (
60
) to expand the L.H.S. of Eq. (
397
), obtaining

\displaystyle\log\max\{(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{1}}},\varepsilon e^{\beta_{h}E_{j}}\}\leq A-\log Z_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}}.

(398)

We know that since near perfect work is extracted,
\varepsilon
is arbitrarily small. This implies that for
\varepsilon
small enough,
\displaystyle\max\{(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{1}}},\varepsilon e^{\beta_{h}E_{j}}\}=(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{1}}}
.

Similarly, one can evaluate Eq. (
393
) to obtain

\displaystyle\log\max\{(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{2}}},\{\varepsilon p_{i}e^{\beta_{h}E_{i}}\}_{i\neq k_{2}}\}

\displaystyle\leq A-\log Z_{\textup{W}}^{\beta_{h}}.

(399)

Note that the maximization in Eq. (
399
) only picks out the maximum value. In particular, denoting
E_{\rm max}
to be the largest energy eigenvalue of the battery, then whenever

(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{2}}}\geq\varepsilon e^{\beta_{h}E_{\rm max}},

(400)

or equivalently

\varepsilon\leq\left[1+e^{\beta_{h}(E_{\rm max}-E_{k_{2}})}\right]^{-1},

(401)

then
\displaystyle\max\{(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{2}}},\{\varepsilon p_{i}e^{\beta_{h}E_{i}}\}_{i\neq k_{2}}\}=(1-\varepsilon)e^{\beta_{h}E_{k_{2}}}
. In other words, as long as
\varepsilon
is upper bounded by Eq. (
401
), we know which terms attains the maximization in Eq. (
398
).
However, we also want an upper bound that is independent of any limit involving the final state
\rho^{1}_{\textup{Cold}\textup{M}\textup{W}}
we wish to take, or any amount of work extracted (and therefore, we want the bound to be independent of
E_{k_{2}}
). As such, let us construct the following upper bound
\varepsilon\leq\hat{\varepsilon}
where,

\hat{\varepsilon}:=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}E_{k_{2}}\\
W^{2}_{\infty}>0\end{subarray}}\left[1+e^{\beta_{h}(E_{\rm max}-E_{k_{2}})}\right]^{-1}=\left[1+e^{\beta_{h}(E_{\rm max}-E_{j})}\right]^{-1}

(402)

Now, we see that
E_{k_{1}}
and
E_{k_{2}}
correspond to the solutions for Eq. (
398
) and Eq. (
399
), which for
\varepsilon\leq\hat{\varepsilon}
reduce to exactly the same equation. Therefore,
E_{k_{1}}=E_{k_{2}}
and hence
W_{\infty}^{1}=W_{\infty}^{2}
.
∎

We will use Lemma
22
to prove Theorem
4
. But before we proceed, let us fix some notation: we define the efficiency as a function of
\alpha\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\eta_{\alpha}^{J}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})=\sup_{E_{k_{J}}-E_{j}>0}\eta(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})\quad\textup{ subject to }

\displaystyle\quad F_{\alpha}(\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}}\otimes\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0},\tau_{\textup{ColdW}}^{h})\geq F_{\alpha}(\rho^{J}_{\textup{W}}\otimes\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1},\tau_{\textup{ColdW}}^{h}),

(403)

and

\displaystyle\quad\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{0})=\textup{tr}(\hat{H}_{t}\rho_{\textup{ColdHotMW}}^{1,J}).

(404)

with
J=1,2
denoting the final battery state
\rho^{J}_{\textup{W}}
. We also define an
\alpha
independent efficiency:

\displaystyle\eta^{J}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})=\sup_{E_{k_{J}}-E_{j}>0}\eta(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})\quad\textup{ subject to }

(405)

\displaystyle F_{\alpha}(\rho^{0}_{\textup{W}}\otimes

\displaystyle\tau_{\textup{Cold}}^{0},\tau_{\textup{ColdW}}^{h})\geq F_{\alpha}(\rho^{J}_{\textup{W}}\otimes\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1},\tau_{\textup{ColdW}}^{h})\,\forall\alpha\geq 0.

(406)

For any
\alpha\geq 0
, and any state
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
,
\eta_{\alpha}^{J}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})\geq\eta^{J}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
holds.

We already know that when
\Omega>1
, for any final cold bath state
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
, the efficiency
\eta^{1}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
is strictly less than the Carnot efficiency. Theorem
4
shows that this is also true for
\eta^{2}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
, i.e. when allowing a more general battery final state.

Theorem 4
.

[General battery states do not improve efficiency] Consider a quasi-static heat engine with a cold bath consisting of
n
qubits, extracting near perfect work. Let
\Omega>1
(definition in Eq. (
269
)). Then the efficiency
\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{2}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})
, where
\tau_{\beta_{f}}
is the final state of a quasi-static heat engine (Def.
6
), is strictly less than the Carnot efficiency.

Proof.

Firstly, suppose that
\Omega>1
. By Lemma
15
we know that the infimum is obtained at
\alpha=\infty
, and by Lemma
16
we know that the efficiency for quasi-static heat engine is strictly less than the Carnot value:

\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{1}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})=\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{1}_{\infty}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})<\eta_{C}.

(407)

In other words, for all other final states
\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
we know that Carnot efficiency cannot be achieved. Therefore, it suffices to see that in the quasi-static limit,

\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{2}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})\leq\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{2}_{\infty}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})=\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{1}_{\infty}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})=\lim_{g\rightarrow 0^{+}}\eta^{1}(\tau_{\beta_{f}})<\eta_{C}.

(408)

The second equality is obtained by noting that for any state
\tilde{\rho}_{\textup{Cold}}^{1}
(and therefore for
\tau_{\beta_{f}}
):

1.

\Delta C
is the same for both expressions of efficiency
\eta^{1}_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
and
\eta^{2}_{\infty}(\rho_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
.

2.

By Lemma
22
, for all
0<\varepsilon<\left[1+e^{\beta_{h}(E_{\rm max}-E_{j})}\right]^{-1}
,
W_{\infty}^{1}(\tilde{\rho}_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})=W_{\infty}^{2}(\tilde{\rho}_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
.

Hence, from Items 1 and 2, one concludes that
\eta^{1}_{\infty}(\tilde{\rho}_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})=\eta^{2}_{\infty}(\tilde{\rho}_{\textup{Cold}}^{1})
.
The third equality in Eq. (
408
) comes directly from Eq. (
407
).
∎
